=== FILE: A Guide to Assistive Equipment for Friedreich’s Ataxia [fnHi48amrJA].txt ===

 if you or a loved one has Friedrich's Ataxia, you know it can make everyday activities challenging. And because F-A is a progressive disease, your needs may change over time. But the right assistive equipment can help you maintain mobility. They can also help you keep up your energy, prevent injuries, and make your day-to-day a little easier. Let's explore some of the most commonly used solutions for people with F-A. Number one, canes. Canes help with balance and stability. They can be especially useful in the early stages of F-A. But getting the right kind for your needs is important. A healthcare provider can help you choose the right size, material, and design. Number two, walkers. Walkers can provide additional support while walking. Standard walkers have rubber-tipped feet for stability. And wheeled walkers and rollators offer easier maneuverability. Which is best for you depends on your level of ability and the specific use. Walkers can provide more stability if you have trouble with balance. Rollators may be a better choice for getting around outside. Number three, wheelchairs. As walking becomes more difficult, a wheelchair can provide reliable mobility. They can be self-propelled or pushed by someone else. Power wheelchairs offer even greater independence and joystick controls. Number four, scooters. Mobility scooters are powered by batteries and have handlebars for steering, making them useful both indoors and out. And many of them can be broken down to transport easily. Scooters can be easier to maneuver than wheelchairs. But a wheelchair may be more comfortable for longer periods of time. Check with your insurance to see if you qualify for a wheelchair or scooter. Number five, home modifications. Inside your home, installing handrails can provide you with support and peace of mind. They can be especially helpful to help you navigate hallways, bathrooms, and around staircases. And stair lifts can help you navigate multiple floors without the risk of falling. Six, adaptive clothing. If your fine motor skills have been affected by FA, dressing yourself can become difficult. The right clothing can make getting dressed easier and more comfortable. Look for shirts with magnetic closures instead of buttons, pants with elastic waistbands instead of zippers, and shoes with fabric fasteners instead of laces. As FA progresses, your needs may change. A healthcare professional, like a physician or physical therapist, can help you find the assistive devices and mobility aids that will work best for you. And a home safety assessment can help you find solutions to keep you safe at home. kab kab kab kab kab kab

=== FILE: Bipolar I vs. Bipolar II： What’s the Difference？ [ddEo6QlKC8s].txt ===

 bi pongizzoar, naringi,adic, naringi,adic, naringi,adic, naringi,adic, naringi,adic, naringi,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic,adic Depression is common in both types. You may lose interest in things you enjoy, feel hopeless, or struggle to get out of bed. For those with Bipolar II, depressive episodes often last longer, sometimes more than two weeks, and may feel more intense than Bipolar I. The key difference lies in mania. Only people with Bipolar I experience mania. Periods of high energy, racing thoughts, or a sense of invincibility. This can lead to risky behaviors like overspending, gambling, and reckless driving. Some may even experience psychosis, which can include delusions or hallucinations. People with Bipolar II experience hypomania instead. These periods aren't as extreme as manic episodes. You may feel unusually happy or energetic, but not experience the delusions or hallucinations that are common with mania. Between mood episodes, many people experience euthymia, a return to their baseline mood. These periods of normalcy can happen in both Bipolar I and Bipolar II. While both types of Bipolar disorder can significantly disrupt someone's life, people with Bipolar I tend to have a hard time maintaining relationships or remaining employed. It can be more difficult to treat Bipolar I and may require hospitalization, since manic symptoms can be dangerous. In Bipolar II, hypomania episodes may not impact someone's life as much. But that means Bipolar II is often misdiagnosed as depression. Treatment for both Bipolar I and Bipolar II is often a combination of medication and therapy. But it's common for people with either type of Bipolar II to have trouble sticking with their treatment program. Bipolar II is lifelong, but it's also manageable. With the right care and support, many people lead healthy, fulfilling lives.

=== FILE: Blood Cancers： What Are They And How Do We Treat Them？ [PH_CK7HzIlU].txt ===

 blood cancer is one of the most common cancers in the world, with 1.2 million people getting diagnosed every year. That might seem dire, but survival rates have steadily increased over the last 20 years, thanks to innovative treatments and ongoing research. There are more than 120 different kinds of blood cancers. The three most common are leukemia, lymphoma, and myeloma, and they directly affect your blood, bone marrow, and lymphatic system. We don't always know the direct cause of blood cancers, but they often occur as a result of having too many abnormal blood cells. These abnormal cells tend to grow at increasing rates, crowding out healthier ones over time. As a result, your bone marrow has a hard time producing the healthy cells needed to perform essential tasks. Different blood cancers have different risk factors, but things like smoking, exposure to chemicals, autoimmune diseases, genetic disorders, and a family history of blood cancers can all increase your risk as you get older. Symptoms vary depending on the kind of blood cancer you have, but some of the more common symptoms include shortness of breath, fatigue, swollen lymph nodes, and frequent infections, night sweats, joint and bone pain, and unexpected weight loss are also possible. You might even experience unexplained bruising, bleeding, or rashes. These symptoms can occur on their own for a variety of other conditions too. So make sure you speak with a healthcare provider who can run blood tests, imaging tests, and other exams to figure out what's causing them. The good news is there are more treatment options today than ever before. Depending on the kind of blood cancer you have, your care team could suggest chemotherapy, radiation therapy, immunotherapy, targeted therapy, stem cell transplants, or even some combination of these. In many cases, these treatments could help manage your blood cancer for years, or even put it into long-term remission. If you are experiencing unusual symptoms, or have a family history of blood cancer, talk with your doctor. Early testing and diagnosis can make a major difference.

=== FILE: Can Prediabetes Be Reversed？ [UaoAcfdY7d4].txt ===

 prediabetes is reversible and you can really take ownership to reverse it. So it may seem daunting, but small, gradual lifestyle changes will make a huge impact. So starting to plan what foods you want available to you in the home and being able to put meals together that are equally balanced. And I know that can be difficult or daunting, but most of the time I see my patients starting their day off with carbohydrates. So they might be eating cereal or toast or bagels, waffles or something very carbohydrate laden and that's only going to cause your blood sugar to spike and then drop and then you're feeling hungry again, your blood sugar is dropping, you need energy. So it only leads to you grabbing those carbs again and it's this vicious cycle throughout the day. If you're not consuming enough protein and healthy fat with your meals, you're going to get into that blood sugar trap of the elevation and then decline and then consistently trying to feed that energy with carbohydrate based foods because carbs are our body's main source of energy and we certainly need them, but it's just the balance. So I educate a lot on providing enough protein with each meal. Starting off your day with protein is ideal because it really kind of starts that blood sugar off in the right level. So protein and healthy fats are essential. That could look like a veggie omelet with some avocado, some fainting Greek yogurt with some berries and nuts on top. So something that is more protein lead in and then you know, kind of continuing that same trend throughout the day. Fiber is essential for slowing down the rate of glucose. So if you are going to eat carbs, you want to choose ones that have high fiber carbohydrates that have fiber tend to have protein. More protein when compared to a simple carbohydrate that does not have fiber. So that is going to be the ideal to balance blood sugars and balance appetite. So I think that the key is that meal timing and the macro nutrient composition of your meals. There is no one size fits all diet and I think that's really important to understand. Yes, I think the Mediterranean diet is great, but really we really need to understand how food affects us, what we're eating and how it's affecting our bodies and how we can change what we're eating to better improve our health outcome. So it can be very specific to you. If there are food choices that you really like and you don't want to give up, we can certainly build those in. It's just a matter of understanding how to build them in, what time to build them in, what to combine them with and how much to eat of them. === FILE: Decongestants vs. Antihistamines [LKqGlolQ_0s].txt ===

 superheroesGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGGG know what they treat. Decongestants are your go-to for that blocked, stuffy feeling. They work best when a cold, sinus infection, or other invader has your nose bound up, but they won't treat the infection. Antihistamines are built to take on your allergy symptoms, like sneezing, runny nose, watery eyes, and itchiness triggered by pollen, dust, or pet dander. And how you use them matters. Decongestants are a short-term fix. You might take them for a few days when your sinuses are on the fritz. They're not meant for long-term use, especially nasal spray decongestants. Using those more than three days straight can actually make your congestion worse. Antihistamines, especially the non-drowsy ones, are often used daily during allergy season to prevent symptoms. Think of them as a strategy for managing your allergies for the long haul. But how do they work their magic? When you have a respiratory infection, the blood vessels in your nasal passage swell. That doesn't leave room for air to flow. Decongestants shrink those blood vessels to open up some space. Antihistamines work by blocking histamine. That's a chemical your body releases during an allergic reaction. But what if it's both? What if you have allergies and a cold? First, our condolences. But no worries. We got you. You can take both or use a combination medicine like Claritin-D or Zyrtec-D, which include an antihistamine and a decongestant. Just check with your provider first, especially if you have high blood pressure, glaucoma, or other health concerns. If you choose a decongestant, check labels before you buy. A medication called oral phenylepharin was once common in over-the-counter decongestants, but it's been judged ineffective by FDA advisors and is being phased out. The more you understand about what's causing your symptoms, the easier it is to find relief. When in doubt, don't guess. Ask a healthcare provider. They'll help you breathe easier. Literally.

=== FILE: Eczema or Psoriasis？ How To Tell the Difference [B04l6dGdgIA].txt ===

! PAMECCAMAM solatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxatxat Your immune system speeds up cell development, causing skin cells to pile on top of one another. This creates dry, scaly plaques with well-defined borders. These plaques can itch, crack, and bleed, and tend to show up more often on the outer edges of your elbows and knees. Psoriasis can also show up on your nails, scalp, lower back, genitals, and your palms or feet. Though it has several causes, it's most often triggered by internal sources, like skin injuries, stress, infections, sudden changes in body temperature, and certain medications. In comparison, itching tends to be more intense with eczema, and it's more commonly caused by external sources, like pet dander, food allergies, and other allergens, smoke and pollution exposure, harsh soaps, and other skin products. Eczema appears most often on the creases of skin, behind your knees, your inner elbows, around your eyes and cheeks, or your neck and hands. And while treatments vary for either condition, both psoriasis and eczema can be difficult to diagnose without a trained healthcare professional. To reach a diagnosis, doctors often look at your family history, since both can be hereditary. It also sometimes helps to have a biopsy when it's hard to visually determine the difference between the two. A good rule of thumb is that if you have a red, itchy rash that doesn't get better with over-the-counter medication and keeps coming back, have a dermatologist take a look to see what's going on under the surface. Have a dermatologist! Thank you.

They just have need of resuscitation and fetal acidosis. So the landscape of how we look at those conditions supported also by animal data showing that mild responds well to HIE. And the fact that our assessment and examination in the first six hours is not that sensitive as far as are we really that good seeing what's mild and what's moderate within six hours when the exam changes all the time, led to seeing in practice registries reporting a significant number of infants with so-called mild HIE receiving hypothermia. It ranges from a quarter of old-cooled infants in the Australian registry all the way to 75% in a UK survey in London that was done about five years ago. So this and the fact that it was so difficult to determine if the mild is going to stay mild or mild become moderate and the fear of litigations led to this creep in practice in babies receiving hypothermia because people were concerned. What if we miss this time zero? What if we miss this time zero? We want to give them every single chance. And the issue is the people that were calling were not comparing to the people were not calling and were not necessarily reporting the two year outcomes of those infants with mild HIE. This led to the randomized controlled trials pilots that were being implemented in the last few years to not be able to succeed in their enrollment. So those pilot studies had about 10 to 20 infants with mild HIE in each arm. The parents were not consenting. And that was because of even the stakeholders were conflicted. So we reported in seminars of fetal neonatal medicine and pediatric research, we and others about this controversy about what best to do and how to handle babies with mild HIE. And this is the exact background that led to the COOL PRIME trial. And I'm excited that we are working diligently, diligently, diligently, diligently now to add Cleveland as our 20th and last site to this trial. I'm happy to share more exercises later, but just kind of like we did good for that first round. The other part of my research is dynamic biomarkers. Separating mild and moderate with the human eye is not easy task. That's why all the trials are complicated. We're very good at what's severe, what's normal. Between mild and moderate, even the typical EEG, amplitude EEG, and NEARS or blood biomarker, they are the best at detecting the very severe. The definition of mild and moderate is so fine that what we need is dynamic progression of biomarkers. Whether it's the way the cerebral blood flow responds to changes in EEG, like the same way we do functional network connectivity in adults, babies don't respond to stimuli. We need to think of other dynamic biomarkers of energy failure and clinical assessments. All those systems have complicated math behind them. They're not steady systems. And it's a thing that can often be in the background for years. Why is it so important? So, um, atrial fibrillation is the most common heart rhythm disturbance that exists in adults. Um, and it represents an irregularity in the top chains of the heart. So the way I sort of think of it is that rather than having a nice organized pattern of electrical activation in those top chains of the heart, which are responsible for governing the heart rhythm, you've got completely chaotic activity. So kind of like a bag of worms, thousands and thousands of electrical signals all whizzing around, interacting with each other and causing chaos. We don't understand the patterns of activation, um, uh, enough to really be able to define them properly, but it's complete chaos. Um, and it has sort of two main consequences. The first is that the, the atria, those top chains of the heart, they don't contract anymore. So they don't form any muscular squeeze, which means that in particular places in the heart, the blood can pool. And if it pools, because it's not flowing so much, it can clot. And this is really leads us to the most important consequence of atrial fibrillation, because if a blood clot forms in the heart and then it moves, it goes into the circulation, it can fly out of the heart and block a blood vessel distal to it. Now, the most serious consequence of that is a stroke. And this is really the most important consequence of atrial fibrillation. It's why we are so focused on, on finding it. Um, strokes associated with atrial fibrillation are a huge problem. About 10% strokes in the UK are because of, um, AF. Um, and as a result, predominantly of the strokes that AF causes, AF as a single condition is responsible for about 1% of the NHS's budget. So this is a vast public health problem. Um, the second consequence of AF is that the heart, because of those chaotic electrical signals arriving, um, at the main pumping chains of the heart, the heart typically goes rapidly and irregularly. And that can cause symptoms of palpitations that can cause heart failure if it's untreated, um, and a whole range of other symptoms that can be really quite impactful, um, for patients. === FILE: How (and Why) To Follow a Healthy MS Diet [0_9B9aWDi40].txt ===

 rationalecccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc with it. Some foods are known to calm inflammation, while others fire it up. The more you can swap out inflammatory foods, the more room you make for inflammation fighters. Another benefit, eating well can help lower your risk for other chronic conditions that make managing MS even harder. That includes things like diabetes, heart disease, and obesity. So where do you start? Foods that can help include fruits and vegetables, whole grains like oats, quinoa, and brown rice, lean proteins like fish, poultry, beans, and nuts, extra virgin olive oil, and low-fat or fat-free dairy. And the foods you want to scale back on? They include red meat, full-fat dairy, fried or processed foods, added sugars, refined carbs like white bread and pasta, and butter, mayo, and creamy salad dressings. Some people find that avoiding or limiting gluten or dairy products helps too, but that's not the case for everyone. If you are looking for a pre-planned diet that can help with MS, check out the Mediterranean diet. It's packed with heart-healthy, anti-inflammatory foods, and is easy to follow. Or talk to a healthcare provider to personalize your plan. There's no perfect MS diet, but there is a perfect strategy. Choose whole, nourishing foods most of the time. Cut back on processed, sugary, and fatty foods. And pay attention to how your body responds.

=== FILE: How Fast Aortic Stenosis Progresses [tfVEinu4HqA].txt ===

 Your heart's aortic valve plays a vital role in how your body works. As blood circulates through your heart, it makes its way to your aortic valve, which allows blood to flow out to the rest of your body. But when you have aortic stenosis, this valve can become narrowed or blocked, typically by a calcium buildup. In some cases, people are born with abnormalities of their valve that may progress stenosis over time. This makes it more difficult for blood to move through. So how quickly does aortic stenosis progress? It can vary from person to person. Your doctor will use tests like a cardiac CT scan or an echocardiogram to determine the stage of your aortic stenosis. Based on your test results, your aortic stenosis may be classified as mild. Your valve opening is more than 1.5 square centimeters. Moderate. Your valve opening is between 1.1 to 1.5 square centimeters. Severe. Your valve opening is less than 1.0 square centimeters. Certain risk factors can contribute to how fast it develops, like being older than 65, having high blood pressure, having high cholesterol, having coronary artery disease, CID, having kidney disease, using tobacco products. Studies show that on average, aortic stenosis gets narrower between 0.1 square centimeters to 0.3 square centimeters every year. There's a chance you could have aortic stenosis and not even know it. You may not have any symptoms until your aortic stenosis progresses to the moderate or severe degree. That's why it's important to talk to your healthcare provider about symptoms like chest pain, fainting, and shortness of breath. You can also be proactive about your heart health to limit how quickly your aortic stenosis advances. Your doctor may recommend following a heart-healthy diet, taking medications or even surgery or non-surgical options like TAVR, Trans-Catheter Aortic Valve Replacement.

=== FILE: Myths vs. Facts About Chronic Immune Thrombocytopenia (ITP) [Du8tWXTEPB4].txt ===

 When it comes to chronic immune, thrombocytopenia, or chronic ITP, there's a lot that people may misunderstand. The rare blood disorder affects how your blood clots. In addition to bruising and bleeding easily, you may notice tiny red or purple dots on your lower legs, known as petechia. And if petechia joined together, those dots may form red, purple, or brown spots on your skin, known as purpura. Let's set the story straight on ITP by busting five common myths. Myth number one, chronic ITP can always be cured. In some cases, ITP goes away on its own or with treatment. In other situations, ITP may become a chronic condition, which means symptoms can be treated but not cured. Myth number two, chronic ITP is a genetic condition. ITP can happen in both children and adults, but there's no genetic link associated with chronic ITP. Myth number three, chronic ITP is a type of cancer. Chronic ITP is a rare blood disorder and isn't considered cancer, though it's often confused with leukemia. Myth number four, chronic ITP is caused by bone marrow abnormalities. If you have chronic ITP, your immune system makes antibodies that incorrectly identify your cells as being invaders. It sends other immune cells to attack your platelets. Myth number five, women who have chronic ITP should avoid pregnancy. Your fetus won't be affected by ITP. Your baby may have fewer platelets at birth, but their platelet count will increase over time. If you do need treatment during pregnancy, your healthcare provider will recommend treatments that won't affect your fetus. If you have chronic ITP, you may need to take medication for the rest of your life. You'll also have to make lifestyle changes, like avoiding contact sports, to reduce your risk of getting hurt. A healthcare provider may also recommend other steps, such as treating the underlying infection, avoiding certain medications, having a platelet transfusion, or even having your spleen removed through surgery. While rare, chronic ITP can be a scary medical diagnosis, especially if you experience bleeding for no reason. But healthcare providers can help treat and manage the condition and offer advice on how to live with chronic ITP.

All righty. This is a key SEBA knowledge. Actually, I would say this is a foundational level knowledge for SEBA and for anybody managing the ventilator. And it's the equation of motion, as many of you recognize. However, I think that what we want to highlight today is to start thinking, and you'll see today the example of what we talk about. What you will see is the equation of motion in action. And let me highlight a couple of items. Whenever we have somebody on mechanical ventilation, the equation of motion tells us that either we control the left side of the equation, so meaning we control the pressure on the ventilator, and so that would be pressure control, right? Or we control the left side, and we're controlling volume and flow, and we call that volume control. And that's relevant because we have repeated an agnoseum that when you're in volume control, you look at the pressure, and when you're in pressure control, you look at the flow and the volume. So important features to recognize. However, it's also important to know that changes either in flow, volume, or pressure are influenced by three things, essentially. concentrate concentrate concentrate concentrate concentrate concentrate concentrate concentrate concentrate which predisposes your risk of developing breast cancer, like a BRCA mutation. Your doctor might recommend getting a double mastectomy as well if you have bilateral breast cancers or breast cancers in both breasts. If you only have breast cancer in one of your breasts and don't carry a gene mutation, then a double mastectomy is not shown to improve your survival. But some women still choose to undergo a double mastectomy out of personal preference. What happens before a double mastectomy? Before surgery, you'll typically meet with your multidisciplinary team to determine whether other treatment is needed before surgery, such as chemotherapy. If you're interested in reconstruction, then your breast surgical oncologist will help refer you to a plastic surgeon to discuss reconstruction options. Reconstruction can include using either an implant or tissue elsewhere in the body. There are several different types of mastectomies that we could offer you, depending on the type of breast cancer you have and your breast size, but to allow for plastics to do reconstruction. A mastectomy that you might talk about includes a skin-sparing mastectomy or a nipple-sparing mastectomy. And this helps create an envelope of skin that allows the plastic surgeon to use to rebuild the breast. What happens during a double mastectomy? During a mastectomy, all of the breast tissue underneath the skin is removed down to the chest wall while preserving the pectoralis major muscle. So depending on where your incision is, whether you're getting a skin-sparing mastectomy or a nipple-sparing mastectomy, the envelope of the skin is used for the plastic surgeon to place a tissue expander, which is a temporary implant, before you get your permanent reconstruction. After complete treatment, your breast surgeon might sample some lymph nodes if you have cancer or any suspicion for cancer being there. And then a temporary surgical drain will be left in place before you leave the operating room. This is typically left in place for about one to two weeks, post-operatively, to prevent fluid from building up underneath the mastectomy flap. What happens after a double mastectomy? Most patients are discharged the same day after their mastectomy, whether they're getting reconstruction or not. Some patients might need a stay for one or two nights after the operation. You'll be prescribed pain medications to help manage your pain at home. You will follow up with your plastic surgeon or your breast surgeon to determine the readiness of your drain and when that will be ready for it to be removed. Typically, we say to plan on having about six to eight weeks for a full recovery after a mastectomy. Most patients can resume going back to work around three to six weeks, and we recommend that you avoid heavy lifting or strenuous activity for about six weeks after the operation. Some patients might benefit from getting physical therapy after your mastectomy, and that referral can be placed after you see us in the office. What are the possible side effects after this procedure? A lot of patients might feel tightness in their chest wall or some nerve pain. That typically improves with time and with more range of motion exercises. Some patients might develop post-mastectomy pain syndrome, and if that's the case, then there are some modalities to try to help with the neuropathic pain. Potential long-term side effects might include lymphedema if lymph nodes are removed at the time of surgery. Typically, when that happens, your breast surgeon will help monitor these symptoms and can refer you to a breast physical therapist or a lymphedema specialist to help with prevention and becoming a further problem. What is the prognosis after a double mastectomy? Your prognosis will depend on what we find at the time of surgery. If there's additional treatment needed, such as chemotherapy, radiation, or endocrine therapy, that will depend on your pathology results and the type of cancer that you have. If you get a double mastectomy to reduce your risk of breast cancer because you have an increased risk of breast cancer from a gene mutation, getting a double mastectomy reduces your risk of developing breast cancer by about 90 to 95%. The decision to undergo a mastectomy is a personal one and your breast surgical oncologist will be there along the way to help you make those decisions. There's different types of mastectomies to undergo and they'll help guide you in getting you an individualized treatment for you.
